le·gy·en pa·ti·en·ti·a — “legyen patientia”
257 Fényes Porta · 277 Vezér · 219|229 A · 192 ge

Constantinople → Ferenc Rákóczi II · Hungarian syllabic nomenclator · 1706–1710

János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, 1706–1710

Eleven despatches of János Pápai to Rákóczi, MNL OL G15 (DECODE R740–R823), catalogued as not or partly decrypted, five of them under other senders. The key table issued to Pápai survives as DECODE R580; applied unchanged, it decrypts 99.7% of the groups in the eleven numeric letters. Ten of them build sentences, but seven of those (R741, R757, R765, R793, R794, R796, R823) read at 88.9–91.4% of their words, under the 95% a complete reading needs; the short cipher passage of R740 is decoded, not read. A twelfth letter, R731, uses a separate alphabet of graphic signs and has its own page.

Daniel Bourdeau · posted 21 September 2026 · updated 26 October 2026

Method: read with known key · Extent: partial (99% of the cipher)

Credits · Contemporary key or decipherment (key or decipherment used)

known key
On this pageThe documentsThe key issued to PápaiWhat the letters sayWhat remains openFull textsSources

At a glanceKnown key applied · partial (99% of the cipher)

The letters
Twelve despatches of János Pápai, Ferenc Rákóczi II’s envoy at the Ottoman Porte, 1706–1710, in Hungarian with some Latin.
Where
Hungarian National Archives key; DECODE R580 (the key), with letters R795, R796, R793, R794, R805, R823, R765, R784, R757, R741, R740 and R731.
The cipher
A Hungarian syllabic nomenclator with values 10–322: two homophones per letter, syllables, and names. Groups above 322 are nulls.
How it was read
Pápai’s own key, R580, applied unchanged: first on R805 (336 of 336 groups), then on the rest. A second pass brings the numeric letters to 16,641 of 16,698 groups (99.7%).
What it says
Imperial troops crossing Ottoman ground, audiences with the Vezír, the French ambassador failing to bring the Porte to war, Muscovy, and money.
Still open
R731 (Belgrade, 1710) hides six short passages in a graphic alphabet, about 330 signs, and no Hungarian came out. 57 digit fragments fit no key value.

The group counts are what the decoder parses from DECODE’s manual transcriptions. Groups without a value are mostly one-digit slips. R823 also opens with a few sums in plain figures (400–1000), which are not cipher.

02 The key issued to Pápai

DECODE R580 (G15 Caps. C Fasc. 43/39) is a Hungarian syllabic nomenclator with values from 10 to 322:

  • Letters, two homophones each: A 219|229, B 209|239, E 179|269, N 100|304, T 79|309, Z 60|312. The same pattern covers ö, ü and the digraphs ny, ly, gy, ty, cz, sz, nk and rt.
  • Syllables in columns by final digit: 11 ab, 21 ad … 192 ge, 245 va … 236 üz.
  • Names: 246 Király, 256 Fejedelem, 257 Fényes Porta, 267 Török Császár, 277 Vezér, 287 Bassa, 297 Bécs. Countries run from 17 to 207; towns, rivers and months from 18 to 298.

The letters have no word division and no nulls. DECODE had R805 as “partially decrypted” from a few interlinear syllables. The first test of the key on R805 gave 336 of 336 groups as running Hungarian, and the other letters followed without any change to the key.

03 What the letters say

The key gives continuous text without word division. The quotations below are from the word-divided readings of 23 October 2026 (texts/R*_reading.md, with English beside them), in the figures’ spelling; for R805 and R795, which have no word-divided reading file yet, word breaks and accents are editorial. Every value is grade H (a direct table value).

  • R805, 1 May 1706. The Porte should refuse the Germans passage across Ottoman ground: az németnek szabados általmenetelt ne engedgye … az Aradi Bassának ordere nélkül meg ne engedgye az németnek Szeged felé való általmenetelt. The envoys have also spoken to the Vezír about Muscovy.
  • R823, 19 Dec 1706. An audience in the presence of the Imperial internuncio, reported partly in Latin: presentia autem vestra non est ingrata Portae. The hereditary-kingship argument follows: imperator: hereditarii reges sunt Angliae, Daniae, Sveciae, et tamen legibus subsunt, with the envoys’ answer that Hungary will not give up its old laws.
  • R765, 5 Jan 1707. Submission to the Prince’s will and the state of Poland. On the Prince’s order the envoys went to the French ambassador: A Francia Ország oratorhoz elmentünk.
  • R795, R796, R793, R794, January–March 1706 (added 21 Sept 2026). Catalogued as two ciphertexts of “Constantinople envoys”, partly deciphered interlinear at the time; they are four despatches of Pápai, decrypted with his key. The check of 23 October 2026 first found R793 and R796 garbled in places; their figures were then re-transcribed on the DECODE images. The decoder had dropped every figure line that also carried an interlinear tag (eight lines of R793), and the transcriber had left out a line of each letter, repeated half a line of R796 and slipped about thirty groups. Corrected and decoded again, both now build sentences beside the transcriber’s copy of the interlinear decipherment; a name on p. 1 of R793, part of its Black Sea passage and the closing lines of R796 stay open, where the figures agree with the image and no key value carries them. R795, signed Constancz die prima Januarii Ezer het szaz hat, Pápai János, written with Ferenc Horváth, reports the Prince’s discretion money handed to the Vezír’s Kiaya, the Tatar Khan, the Pasha of Temesvár and a new route for letters through Moldavia. R796 (20 February) answers the Prince’s letter from the Tisza: the Germans spread false news, the confederates are scorned, the Muscovite envoy is at the Porte and nothing is resolved on the propositions. R793 (March) reports secret talks with the Porte’s chancellor on the Muscovite quarrel, the King of Sweden and a Hungarian confederation with the Porte certis conditionibus, and the Prince’s manifesto translated and given to the Vezír. R794 (undated) concerns recruiting horse in Wallachia and the French King’s money, which the ambassador will not pay without the King’s order. In R795 every group above 322 is a null.
  • R784, 1707 (added 21 Sept 2026). Catalogued as “unknown sender to Rákóczi”. Unsigned and undated, but in Pápai’s key, which decrypts it unchanged. A Francia Ország oratori jelentette [?mirtt], hogy semmi ratitiökkal nem inducálhattya Fényes Portát az hadakozásra: the French ambassador reported that no arguments could bring the Porte to war. The grandees of the last war have sworn to see that the Sultan resolves on it, and the whole Turkish nation leans that way. France means to turn Turkey wholly against the Germans (ezt a Török Országot egészen a német ellen fordítsa), from which Hungary would draw harm rather than profit. The ambassador has written the same to the Tatar Khan, and if Muscovy and Poland do not second it, the Germans will overrun Hungary. A note on p. 2 in another hand asks the Prince to keep the letter secret and decipher it himself (… titokban tartani s maga decifrálni).
  • R757, 16 May 1708. A message from the Muscovite ambassador, and Pápai explains why he cannot send money from Constantinople.
  • R740, 2 Jan 1710. The Kiaya’s answer, which is the only enciphered passage: legyen patientiával, ne siessen, várakozzék … ha a Fényes Porta szívére nem veszi … ne kellyen illyen haszontalan költeni s alkalmatlankodni F[elsé]gdnek a Fényes Portán. That is, the Prince should be patient and stop spending money and effort at the Porte to no purpose. The passage is only 168 groups and its middle does not build (half its sentences hold): the transcription agrees with the image, the slips are the writer’s, and no other text of it is known, so R740 is decoded, not read (check of 23 October 2026).
  • R741, 8 May 1710. A visit to the Defterdar. The Pasha of Belgrade’s letter had concerned only the merchants’ business.

04 What remains open

  • R731 (Belgrade, 6 March 1710) is in a graphic-sign alphabet, not this key: see its own page.
  • 57 groups in the eleven numeric letters still have no value (decode2.py, retry_groups.tsv). Scored in context against every key value, none wins clearly. About 41 more tokens are marked “?” in DECODE’s transcriptions.
  • R740: the middle of the 168-group passage, decoded, not read (the writer’s slips). R793: a name on p. 1 and parts of the Black Sea passage; R796: the closing lines before the signature.
  • Measured extent (24 October 2026). Counted word by word on the word-divided readings, with the open groups and doubtful words as holes, seven letters build coherent sentences but read under the 95% a complete reading needs: R793 91.4%, R765 89.5%, R796 89.2%, R823 89.1%, R794 89.0%, R757 89.0%, R741 88.9%.
  • Prior print. Kálmán Benda’s edition of Pápai’s Turkish diaries (1963) was not checked for these despatches.

Image rights. No manuscript image is reproduced. DECODE states: “The image is not in the public domain. Publishing it is only possible with the permission of the archive.”

Full texts

The transcription and the decryption of every document on this page, so that the reading can be used and checked. Short files are shown here; all are in the repository.

R805, 1 May 1706, Constantinople decoded, not read

The envoys urge that the Porte refuse the Imperial troops free passage across Ottoman ground, and that the border pashas, the Pasha of Arad among them, not allow the Germans passage towards Szeged without orders; they have spoken to the Vezír about their affairs and about Muscovy.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

R795, 1 Jan 1706, Constantinople decoded, not read

Pápai with Ferenc Horváth reports the Prince's discretion money handed to the Vezír's Kiaya, the Tatar Khan, the French ambassador, the Pasha of Temesvár, a Croatian incursion, and letters to go via Moldavia so that the Prince's news arrives faster.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription: DOC_R795_D2405_2405.txt

-- groups 1617, in key 1356 (83.9%)

R796, 20 Feb 1706, Constantinople read in part

The Prince's undated letter from the Tisza received; German false news believed neither by the Turks nor the confederates; the plea for those trapped in Moldavia blocked by the armistice articles, so the Moldavian kapukehaya and the serdar of Babadag (former pasha of Belgrade) are to be used; the Porte, frightened by the last war, will not prejudice the armistice nor help the Prince; nothing resolved on the propositions. Constantinople, 20 Feb 1706.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

R796_reading.md

# R796 — János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 20 February 1706: reading

Re-read 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, batch r2g) on the figures. The digits were re-transcribed against the four DECODE
images (IMG_R796_I4453-4456, fetched with the project cookie into the session scratchpad only):
`R796_transcription_corrected.txt` (changes in `R796_corrections.tsv`), decoded with decode2.py's rules
(`python research/reading_audit/r2g_papai_dec.py targets/papai1706/texts/R796_transcription_corrected.txt --map=87=ar`),
and read word by word beside the transcriber's copy of the contemporary interlinear decipherment (`<PLAINTEXT>` lines of
`../DOC_R796_D2406_2406.txt`, lines 1-16 and a few words later; `v4_papai_lines.py`).
The 23 Oct "lines 16-19 repeat letters" was a transcription fault: FK's lines 17-18 (p. 1, ll. 17-18) copied the
second half of l. 18 twice and dropped l. 17; on the image they read *…ar ticulusa praeiudicál, úgy látom difficilisek
ebben fermánt adgyanak az vaidának…*.
Look-alike alternates: 87 read as 81 (*ar*) four times (*armistitium* ×3, *articulus*; the key's 87 = Moskva Ország
makes no sense before *-mistitium*), 187 as 181 (*bü*) once (the interlinear decipherer also reads *-bül*): 10 of
about 1,330 letters (0.75%). Spelling as the figures give it; `{name}` codes written as the names. Clear passages
written in the letter (in Hungarian/Latin, not cipher) are not part of the reading body; see the end.
Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[260]` an unread group, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

Kegyel[mes] uram! Nagod datum nélkül az Tisza mellül küldött [?m] levelét tizenegyedik p[rae]sentis tartozó
engedelmességgel vettem. […] Az levelekkel, mellyekbül az eddig folyt dolgokat Nagod bövön megértheti. Ami penig az
németh hamissan spargált híreit illeti, mivel mi sem mulattyuk el kötelességünket, véghez vittük: nem tsak az török
nem hitte, de meg az confoederatusis contemnálták, tudván azonkívül is, hogy sok hazugsággal szoktak kevés
[?igasságát] dolgainknak szaporetani. Nem mulattuk el instálni az Moldvában szorultak mellett, de mivel az
armistitium[k] articulusa[k] piudicál, úgy látom difficilisek ebben fermánt adgyanak az vaidának; mindazonáltal
továbbra is nem szünöm meg [?…]alnom ezen instantiát, vagy tsak itt az moldvai kapiuta által legyen parancsolattya
az vaidának, kit is az [260] [?ga] principálisának megírde[?m]; tsak Nagod legyen correspondenter addig is azon
vaidával. Tudom, úgy is azon bujdosókat patrocinálja; tannál jobb lesz, ha Nagod az babadaki szerdárnak írna, az
aki Nándorfejérvári basa volt, mivel onnand megváltoztattattak, Babadakra küldték, melly is Moldvára parancsol.
Az erdélyi [?lavetek] bejöveteleket nem [?art], [?tsak] hoc statu rerum admittállyák, mivel az török magaénak
tartván Erdélyt. El nem mulattuk premálni az békességnek [?tractáival] Fényes Portát, továbbra is premállyuk; de meg
kell vallani ezt: az nemzetet soha az német úgy meg nem iesztette, mint az elmúlt hadakozásnak alkalmatosságával;
azért is illy difficilis, nem kívánna örömest piudicálni az armistiumk. Másként is sem Vezér, sem Török
Császár nem [?martis] [?ei] emberek, hanem ad delitias azon jó híreket [?megbeszéllettes] kelletnek; ha Nagod az
Vezér cancellariusnak, muphtinak [?skeletnek] is ír[na] armistitium[k] megállását. Mindazonáltal gondolattyabb
se legyen Nagodnak, hogy az Fényes Porta segítséget adgyon neki, mivel természete szerént gyűlöli maga ellen
jövendőre is, nem kívánnya stabiliálni az beadott propositiókra. Mindeddig is jó lehetott [?…] kelletet, de semmi
resolutio nincs; [?…] tsak reménség is [?…] [?mit] [?adtit] [?kapn] vagyon. Én penig [?maradok] Nagodnak
[?alázatos] szolgája, Pápai János. Constancz, Ezer [?hét] száz hat, huszadik Februarii.

[P.S.] [?Kapitihát] Arnót Országba küld[?ve] Horvát Ferencz urral [?…] maradna menni; valaki itt nem [?maradó].

## Notes on the figures

- l. 6 *mellyekbül*: 40 22 187 119 = ly ek {Bavaria} l; 187 read as 181 (bü), as the interlinear decipherer did.
- ll. 10-11 *véghez vittük*: 255 189 262 60 265 79 175 (ve d he z vi t tü), *d* for *g* as written.
- l. 14 *kevés [?igasságát]*: 139 182 45 182 45 182 79 (i ga sa ga sa ga t): the clerk repeated *saga*.
- ll. 17-19 (corrected on the image): 203 79 201 283 255 89 111 87 283 80 79 113 139 70 109 129 | 87 135 199 70 119 215 41 90
  139 70 211 249 51 70 39 | 193 145 109 211 132 199 63 103 22 261 209 100 — *de mivel az armistitium[k] articulusa[k]
  piudicál [praeiudicál], úgy látom difficilisek ebben*. Writer's spellings kept unbracketed where the figures give them: *piudicál(ni)*, *armistiumk*, *magaénak*, *lehetott*, *tannál*.
- l. 25 *az [260] [?ga] principálisának*: 260 has no key value.
- p. 2 l. 8 *bejöveteleket nem [?art]*: 59 255 125 203 129 54 109 87 79 — the last 87 79 (ar t) as at the other 87s.
- p. 2 l. 12 *békességnek [?tractáival]*: 54 301 79 | 264 199 115 139 279 245 — the image has 301 (FK: 109 14 193);
  279 (F) is not *i*.
- p. 3 l. 4 *armistitium[k] megállását*: 87 283 80 135 135 70 109 129 273 159 … (the image has 135 twice; FK once).
- p. 3 l. 12-13: 308 219 39 79 45 129 274 273 100 55 159 103 75 245 209 283 79 21 — *[?…] tsak reménség is [?…]*;
  the image has 45 and 100 (FK omitted 45 and wrote 200).
- Signature: 20 124 182 139 219 194 334 382 194 139 219 74 80 (334, 382 nulls): *szolgája Pá[pa]i János*.
- p. 3 l. 15 / 18: 224 for *d* in *maradok*, 174 for *a* in *alázatos*: the figures do not give these; bracketed.

## Clear passages in the letter (not cipher; read on the image, doubtful words [?])

The letter has clear Hungarian/Latin passages between cipher lines (p. 1 after l. 4 and l. 18; p. 2 after l. 10 and l.
17; p. 3 ll. 3-8 and the P.S.). FK's transcription of them is often wrong (e.g. "Kivet Béesbril jö mem Solemmis Legarivval"; the image has
*követ*, *Bécsbül*, *solemnis* there). Not transcribed in full here.
Translation

R796_en.md

# R796 — English (János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 20 February 1706)

Same holes as R796_reading.md. Modern spelling; codes as names.

Gracious lord! I received with due obedience on the eleventh of this month Your Highness's letter sent undated from by
the Tisza. […] From the letters with it Your Highness can understand at length how matters have gone so far. As for the
false news spread by the Germans, since we have not neglected our duty either, we have carried it out: not only did the
Turk not believe it, but even the confederates despised it, knowing besides that they are wont to multiply the scant
[?truth] of their affairs with many lies. We did not fail to plead for those trapped in Moldavia; but since it
prejudices the articles of the armistice, I see they are reluctant to give a firman to the voivode about it;
nevertheless I shall not cease to [press] this request further, or at least that an order be given to the voivode
through the Moldavian kapukehaya here, whose [260] I have written to his principal; only let Your Highness meanwhile
keep in correspondence with that voivode. I know he protects those exiles anyway; it will be all the better if Your
Highness writes to the serdar of Babadag, who was pasha of Belgrade, since they were moved from there and sent to
Babadag, and he commands Moldavia.

The coming in of the Transylvanian [envoys?] they do not [?…]; [only] in the present state of things do they admit them,
since the Turk regards Transylvania as [his own]. We did not fail to press the Sublime Porte with [the treaties?] of
peace, and we press it still; but it must be admitted that the German never frightened this nation as much as on the
occasion of the last war; that is why it is so reluctant, and does not willingly wish to prejudice the armistice.
Besides, neither the Vizier nor the Turkish Emperor are [martial?] men, but [take] such good news for their pleasure
[…]; if Your Highness writes to the Vizier's chancellor and to the mufti too, [about] the standing of the armistice.
Nevertheless let Your Highness not think that the Sublime Porte will give him help, since by its nature it hates [the
idea] against itself for the future too, and does not wish to settle on the proposals submitted. Until now it has been
good […], but there is no resolution; […] only hope too […]. And I remain Your Highness's humble servant, János Pápai.
Constantinople, one thousand [seven] hundred and six, twentieth of February.

[P.S.] [The kapukehaya?] sent to Albania with Mr Ferenc Horváth […] would remain to go; whoever does not stay here […].

R793, 16 Mar 1706, Constantinople read in part

Secret approaches to the Porte through the chancellor and the mufti about the unratified Muscovite peace and the Tana (Azov) frontier; with Sweden on the other side Poland could not hold out, and the Emperor is tied down by the French and Hungarian war; the Muscovite advances among the Astrakhanians, Circassians and Mingrelians and on the Black Sea; the Vizier asks whether the Hungarians would ally with Sweden; the Prince's manifesto translated and given to the Vizier; the envoy will pay nothing. Constantinople, 16 Mar 1706.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (91% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

R793_reading.md

# R793 — János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 16 March 1706: reading

Re-read 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, batch r2g) on the figures. The digits were re-transcribed against the four DECODE
images (IMG_R793_I4441-4444, fetched with the project cookie into the session scratchpad only):
`R793_transcription_corrected.txt` (changes in `R793_corrections.tsv`), decoded with decode2.py's rules
(`python research/reading_audit/r2g_papai_dec.py targets/papai1706/texts/R793_transcription_corrected.txt --map=87=ar`),
and read word by word beside the transcriber's copy of the contemporary interlinear decipherment (`<PLAINTEXT>` lines of
`../DOC_R793_D2408_2408.txt`, which cover p. 1 ll. 1-5 and a few words later). The main repairs: decode2.py skipped
every transcription line that also carried a `<PLAINTEXT>` tag (eight lines of figures, among them *az ombn az arrul
való proiectumunkat*, *gyarökkal confoederállyon*, the Tana frontier); split and joined groups were re-spaced (309
for 909, 102 for 2102, 15 for 11 5, 253 100 inserted, …); one line of p. 4 that FK left out was transcribed; the date
line has 98 (Constancz) which FK dropped. The 23 Oct "Muscovite frontier / Black Sea passages garbled" were these
transcription faults.
Look-alike alternates: none used (no 87 in this letter). Spelling as the figures give it; `{name}` codes written
as the names. Clear passages written in the letter are not part of the reading body.
Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

Kegyelmes uram! [?Al…] Nagodat [?…], hogy gyakran nem tudósíthatom, nem restségemnek, hanem az bizonyos
alkallmatosság nemlétének tulaidonítsa. [?Ollehet] [?laholmoggyát] helyét az hozzánk való propensiót tapasztallyuk,
nem szününk meg Nagod parancsolattyát [?…], de mindeddig is elkezdett [?dolgaink] valóságára nem juthattunk. Az
elmúlt napokban [?uj] [?írásökkal] aggrediáltuk az Fényes Portát, mellyet is két részben praesentáltunk: edgyiket az
muszka dolgában, de igen titkon az cancellárius és mufti által, valamint kellett, [?hogy] eiusdem religionis consors
észre ne vegye; mellyre az Fényes Porta [?…] nevü [?…] megjövén az muszkák és az Fényes Porta között fenforgó
[?határi] kigazításárul referálta meg az Tana[?i] mellett való határ dolgát, [?em] nem igazodott, kinek adta [?…] az
orator; meg egészen nem ratificáltotott az muszka Királlyal való békesség. Fényes Portának bele kapni, mivel
egyfelül az Fényes Porta, másfelül az Svéciai Király ha aggrediállya, Lengyel Országnak nem subsistálhatna; nem levén
most az Római Császár dolga az Fényes Portánk egész erövel agálhatna is ellene; sőt ha az Római Császár akarna is
valamit tentálni, az franciákkal s magyarökkal való hadakozása [?…]na; és ha Fényes Portánk az magyarökkal
confoederállyon, az magyarökkal confoederatus levén Svéciai Király, hogy az Fényes Portával confoederállyon certis
conditionibus, Fejedelem Rákóczi véghez viszi.

Fényes Portának már [?…] [?bír] van az muszka az békesség színe alatt az astracanusokat, cserkeszeket, mingrelliosokat
[?…]; az [?kekk] közé már is két száz papokat küldött meg fegyveresen [?…], oda hadgya azon ostiumot, mellyet az
Fényes Porta [?…] ellen elég portus levén Circassia, Mingrellia felül az fekete tengeren immediate Constancz ellen
egyfelül, másfelül penig azon [?…] fekete tengerben [?…] az moldvaial az Római Császár [?…] confoederatusok
concursusok is. Midön ezt megértették, a retraita[?t] az Vezért, úgy hogy észre ne vegye, informáltotása az Vezér
mind ezekrül úgy moderálta opinióit, írásban praesentáltak. Itt tsak hamar hívan az Vezér kérdette tölem: gondolod-e,
hogy az magyarök svecus[sal] confoederálnának? Ebben velök [?…] de hiszem nekik is hasznok forog benne. Azomban az
arrul való proiectumunkat az cancellárius az Vezérnek megértette, hogy mind Nagod, mind az erdélyi Király Nagod
interpositiója által [?…] az Fényes Portával confoederálni kívánnak, másikat más ratiókkal, mellyek is demonstrálták
az Nagod Lengyel Ország[ban] leendö confoe[derati]ó[?ját] [?…] abbul következendö károkat az Fényes Portának. Ezt
penig kellett által[a], manifestumal együtt, mellyek is már interpretáltottan adtuk az Vezérnek. Maga is az orator
az Vezérhez persvadeállya. A reménlünk rövid nap kívánatos választ; ha ezen két rendbeli írásim nem inducállyák,
annak utánna nem látunk mást. Örömöst barátkoznának velünk, az mint az cancellárius szavaibul [?…] ki is, tsak
hogy az német is igen megvetve [?…] annival [?inkább], hogy Erdélyt most az német viszsza foglalta. Az kik Moldvában
vadnak, az ökrül írt az [?…] az vaidának; addig is spatiállya, míg az fermán el megyen. Inclinálnak hozzánk az Vezér
is, impetrálhossuk az orator által, ha szintén praeiudicális az pacificatiónak is. Pénzrül az orator egy pénzt sem ad.
Én penig maradok Nagodnak alázatos szolgája, Pápai János. Constancz, tizenhatodik Martius, Ezer hé[t száz] ha[t].

## Notes on the figures

- p. 1 l. 1: 133 39 32 273 80 205 61 51 44 212 189 101 282 39 — *Kegyelmes uram! al Nagodat hogy*; the interlinear
  decipherer: *Kegyelmes uram alnagodat hosy*. The clear words written on the line (FK: "tröben? Alätton hém?") are
  under a folded strip on the DECODE image; the opening (*Alázatosan kérem?*) is not read.
- p. 1 ll. 9-10: *dorgaink* (221 = do, 89 = r), *uil biras ökkal*: written so; bracketed.
- p. 1 l. 12 (a `<PLAINTEXT>` line, skipped before): 274 20 209 100 90 55 304 115 119 155 19 271 39 53 92 111 14 20 123 —
  *részben praesentáltunk edgyiket az muszka*.
- p. 1 l. 17: 111 257 83 159 65 54 214 212 223 90 54 16 273 159 139 59 255 100 — *az Fényes Porta ing si ne pi go lo p
  ne vü meg i jö ve n*: a name (*[?…] nevü*), not read.
- p. 1 l. 19: 111 115 44 103 273 119 203 79 — *az Tana is mellett* (Tana = the Azov frontier); FK had 11 5 for 115.
- p. 2 ll. 38-42: *mingrelliosokat* (213 164 = li os); *iptofoedere*, *for{Maros}ficentasak*: not read.
- p. 2 l. 47: 111 144 2070 39 71 … — *azon [s zu] gyan*: 2070 is not a key value; read as 20 70, not a word.
- p. 3 ll. 56-60 (one of them skipped before): *Ebben velök olo- de hiszem nekik is hasznok forog benne az ombn az arrul
  való proiectumunkat az cancellárius*.
- p. 4 l. 15 (left out by FK): 129 282 60 26 19 111 277 [clear: *parancsolattyát*] 103 73 204 79 264 119 282 80 —
  *khozzánk az Vezér is impetrál hos-*.
- Date: 98 135 36 100 252 79 99 189 53 178 298 262 288 252 209 — *Constancz tizenhatodik Martius Ezer he[t] Száz ha[t]*
  (209 = B, the last letter not read; the year from the docket and the series).

## Clear passages in the letter (not cipher; read on the image, doubtful words [?])

Short clear passages run between the cipher (p. 1 ll. 1, 7, 9, 11; p. 2 ll. 3, 12, 13, 15-16, 26; p. 3 ll. 3, 11, 13,
15; p. 4 ll. 1, 3, 6, 10, 12-13, 15, 18-20). FK's copies of them are poor (e.g. "Nembetimk? Boloogn? Lásaban" for
p. 1 l. 7). Not transcribed in full here.

R793_en.md

# R793 — English (János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 16 March 1706)

Same holes as R793_reading.md. Modern spelling; codes as names.

Gracious lord! [I humbly ask?] Your Highness to put it down not to my sloth that I cannot report often, but to the lack
of a sure opportunity. [Though?] we find [?…] a disposition towards us, we do not cease [to carry out] Your Highness's
orders, but so far we have not been able to bring our affairs begun to effect. In recent days we approached the
Sublime Porte with [new writings], which we presented in two parts: one on the Muscovite business, but very secretly,
through the chancellor and the mufti, so that, as was necessary, his co-religionist should not notice; upon which
[…], having arrived, reported on the settlement of the frontier disputed between the Muscovites and the Sublime Porte,
on the frontier by the Tana [Azov]: it has not been settled, […] the envoy; the peace with the Muscovite Tsar has not
been fully ratified. [It is time] for the Sublime Porte to take a hand, since with the Sublime Porte on one side and the
King of Sweden on the other, if he attacks, Poland could not hold out; the Roman Emperor's affairs being such that he
could not act against our Sublime Porte with all his strength; indeed, if the Roman Emperor wanted to attempt
something, his war with the French and the Hungarians [would prevent it]; and if our Sublime Porte allies itself with
the Hungarians, the King of Sweden being an ally of the Hungarians, Prince Rákóczi will bring it about that he allies
himself with the Sublime Porte on certain conditions.

The Muscovite, under colour of the peace, already [holds?] the Astrakhanians, the Circassians, the Mingrelians […] of
the Sublime Porte; among the [Kakhetians?] he has already sent two hundred priests, armed […], he leaves that mouth
[of the river] there, which [threatens] the Sublime Porte, there being harbour enough in Circassia and Mingrelia on the
Black Sea, directly against Constantinople on one side, and on the other in that […] Black Sea […] with Moldavia, the
Roman Emperor [and] the confederates' gatherings too. When they understood this, the Vizier, informed of the
withdrawal without its being noticed, so moderated his views on all this; they presented it in writing. Here the
Vizier soon called me and asked me: do you think the Hungarians would ally themselves with the Swede? In this with
them […], but I believe they too have an interest in it. Meanwhile the chancellor explained our project on this to the
Vizier: that both Your Highness and the Transylvanian King, through Your Highness's mediation, wish to ally with the
Sublime Porte, the other with other reasons, which demonstrated the harm to the Sublime Porte that would follow from the
confederation to be made in Poland. This had to be [delivered] with the manifesto, which we gave to the Vizier already
translated. The envoy himself is persuading the Vizier. We hope for the desired answer in a few days; if these two
writings of mine do not move them, after that we see nothing else. They would gladly be friends with us, as [appears]
from the chancellor's words, except that the German too [is] much despised […], all the more since the German has now
retaken Transylvania. Those who are in Moldavia, about them [the envoy] wrote to the voivode; he is waiting meanwhile
until the firman goes off. The Vizier too inclines towards us; we shall obtain it through the envoy, even if it
prejudices the pacification. About money: the envoy gives not a penny. And I remain Your Highness's humble servant,
János Pápai. Constantinople, the sixteenth of March, one thousand seven hundred and six.

R794, undated, early 1706 read in part

Propositions on recruiting four thousand horse in Wallachia, the memorial on Muscovy, and the (French) King's money that the ambassador will not pay without the King's order.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# R794 — János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, [Constantinople, early 1706]: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R794_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription, groups above 322 as nulls), word-divided, and the transcriber's copy of the contemporary interlinear
decipherment (`<PLAINTEXT>` lines of `../DOC_R794_D2404_2404.txt`; it runs four lines ahead of the decode after the
signature block and drifts by one more line from decode line 20). Where the decode is garbled and the interlinear copy
gives the word (*harmincz*, *szükséges*, *pompa nélkül*, *promotione rerum hungaricarum*, *tíz ezer*, *Nagod számára*),
the interlinear is followed: it is a contemporary text. Spelling as the figures give it; nothing modernised. Marks:
`[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[200]` an unread group, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

[…] mind olly kezdeteek, mellyek Nagod kívánságit consumálni fogiák; ki[t] adgyon Isten! [200] […] Pápai János.

[P.S.] Kelletnek az propositiókat [?…] mind Havasalföldön, [?…] tsak hogy az itt az hadfogadást megengedik, mellyet
[?…] mi is négyezer lovassnak fogadása sokban telik, mivel elöre annak tiszteinek külön, az [?gregariusnak] kel
harmincz tallér discretio. Mivel penig azonkívül is Nagodnak elég lovas hada vagyon, a gyalog inkáb szükséges. Az
midön beadgyuk az Moskva Országrul való memoriálisunkat az Vezérnek, sem lovasrul sem gyalogrul nem teszünk
specificatiót, hanem tsak [?…] kívántunk engedelmet fogadásra; és ha Nagod gyalogot többet kívántok, könnyü lészen,
akar lovastak, akar gyalogot. [?…] pénzrül hamar dispositiót tenni, mivel az oratornál nem lészen, a nélkül semmire
nem megyünk az had fogadásban.

Mint kegyelmes uramnak alázatoson jelentem, hogy [?el…] Nagodat az fenforgó dolgokrul informálhassam: az midön ezelött
egynehány nappal az szolgámat az moldvai kalarasokkal [?megindétani] akartam, már szállásokra is elküldöttem valamelly
[?boért], megfogván az adóiért; mindeddig szolgámnak is itt kellett várakozni. [?…] Tegnap igen titkoson jó reggel
küldötte fia, az ki most tolmács az Vezér kapujára, [?hívatván] magamat; kívánta az [?…] levén az rendi, elsöbben az
Vezér tihaiával szemben [?…] igen titkoson, [?mondén] pompa nélkül, mivel úgy kívánta s kértem, szerezzen audientiát
az Vezérnél; obligállya igen az Nagod [7] is, hogy a mikor az Vezértül elbúcsúzván, estve felé mentem az oratorhoz.
Kértem, az dolgok indulnak ennek utánna az discretióra s [?…] az albánusok [?által] török fogadásra, és pénz
kívántotik; mellyet kire felelte: hat [?…] reá assignáltotik az Nagod számára az Királytul járandó pénz, ordere [?…]
készpénzt adni, de a nélkül az magáét nem adhattya, nem a promotione rerum hungaricarum több[et] költvén tíz ezer
tallérnál az magáébul, azt sem refundálta az Király; magát penig, hogy adóssá s koldussá tegye, nem kívánhattyuk;
jóllehet [?valamit] Nagod számára erogál, bizony refundáltotik, de az Király ordere nélkül nem adhat, [?nem] három ezer
tallérig [?…] [?securál], az mellybül az discretio sem telik ki. [?Közelisít] felek az pénz nem léte miatt [?kár]
confusio esik dolgainkban. [?…] Kassa[…] szolgálnak, [?ü] kellett hivatva [?ö] magához az [?Vezér] parancsolattyábul;
[?mondotta], az melly propositióra való dolgok vadnak, írásban tegyem, le vigyem hozzá, had fordétsa törökre úgy
[?…] [?…] holnap [?…] hozzá.

R794_en.md

# R794, Pápai to Rákóczi, early 1706: English

Translation of `R794_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes.

## Translation

[…] all such beginnings as will fulfil Your Highness's wishes; may God grant it! [200] […] János Pápai.

[P.S.] The propositions must [?…] all in Wallachia, [?…] only that they allow the recruiting here, which [?…] the
recruiting of four thousand horse costs us a great deal, since beforehand thirty thalers' discretion must go to its
officers separately, [?and] to the [?common soldiers]. But since Your Highness has enough cavalry besides, foot is more
needed. When we hand the Vizier our memorial about Muscovy, we make no specification either of horse or of foot, but
only asked permission to recruit; and if Your Highness wants more foot, it will be easy, horse or foot. [?…] to make a
disposition about money quickly, since the ambassador will not have it; without it we get nowhere in the recruiting.

As I humbly report to my gracious lord, so that I may inform Your Highness of the matters in hand: when some days ago I
wanted to send my servant off with the Moldavian couriers, I had already sent to the lodgings for some boyar, seized
for his tax; my servant too has had to wait here until now. [?…] Yesterday, very secretly, early in the morning, he
sent his son, who is now interpreter at the Vizier's gate, summoning me; he wished, [?…] the order being, first to meet
the Vizier's kiaya [?…] very secretly, [?…] without pomp, since he so wished, and I asked him to obtain an audience with
the Vizier; he much obliges Your Highness's [7] too, so that when, having taken leave of the Vizier, I went towards
evening to the ambassador, I asked: the affairs are now starting, money is needed for the discretion and for
recruiting Turks [?through] the Albanians; to which he answered: [?…] the money due from the King for Your Highness is
assigned to it, [?but without] his order [?he cannot] give cash, and without it he cannot give his own, having spent
more than ten thousand thalers of his own not for the promotion of Hungarian affairs, and the King has not refunded
even that; and we cannot want him to make himself a debtor and a beggar; though what he spends for Your Highness is
surely refunded, without the King's order he cannot give, [?not] up to three thousand thalers [?…], from which the
discretion is not even covered. [?…] through lack of money a damaging confusion falls on our affairs. [?…] Kassa [?…]
serve; he had to be summoned to him by the [?Vizier's] order; he [?said] I should put in writing the matters relating
to the propositions and take them to him, so that he may have them turned into Turkish [?…] tomorrow [?…] to him.

R823, 19 Dec 1706, Constantinople read in part

Report of an audience at the Porte in the presence of the Imperial internuncio, partly in Latin, with the argument over hereditary kingship and the envoys' reply that Hungary cannot give up its old laws.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# R823 — János Pápai and Ferenc Horváth to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 19 December 1706: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R823_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription; in R823 the 400-1000 figures are clear numerals, not nulls), word-divided. No interlinear decipherment.
Hungarian and Latin as the figures give them (*conversatur*, *praesentia* written *presentia*; *öknak*, *királyök*);
nothing modernised; codes written as names. Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[7]` an
unread group, `[x]` a letter supplied. The opening figures 476 … 772 are a sum in clear numerals and are left out.

## Reading

[…] [?Portugalli] [numerals] […] Király elmenvén, azonnal insinuáltuk magunkat, s kérlet nélkül [?itt] admittáltván
duodecima p[rae]ntis, jelentettük hoszszas várakozásunkat, kértük dolgaink promotióját. Arra felelt: [?Magyar Ország],
emlékezzem szavaimra, melyeket ezelött mondottam vo[bi]s, scilicet non libenter conversatur vobiscum Fényes Porta in
presentia internuncii; presentia autem vestra non est ingrata Portae. De bizonyos, ujjobban tracta[l] Fejedelem a
némettel: venerunt enim e Vienna [?…] ut de novo colloverentur de illis [?…] propositionibus, quas de novo presentavit
archiepiscopus Kalocensis; ita vos cogitatis esse in utilitatem rerum vestrarum etiam ulterius hic manere, nemo vos
impedit. Mondottuk: ebben nincsen semmi hírünk. Kérdezte: vettük-e levelét Nagodnak? Mondottuk: nem, mivel a minap is
a Nándorfejérvári Basa [?czuhodári] [?…] Drinápolyig hozván a Nagod levelét, 465 [?…] urának [?…] viszszatért, s azt is
viszsza[?…] Nándorfejérvárat is impediállyák. A mint [?…] in discursu, hozta elö: a lám a Magyarságnak nem annyira
kellene irtózni a hereditástul, tantummodo esset sub legibus; imperator: hereditarii reges sunt Angliae, Daniae,
Sveciae, et tamen legibus subsunt; a Magyar Ország is constringálná törvénnyel, ha örökös Király lenne, et sic vos
fructum [?…] teneretis. Feleltünk: uram, más az öknak, az országöknak s királyöknak állapottya: mindenik az ök közzül
magok nemzetébül álló Király, egy coalescált nemzetnek [?parancsol], törvényesen igazgat, nem törvénytelen, de
[?Josephus] német, s mi magyarök vagyunk; Magyar Országon kívül is nagy birodalma vagyon; in casu [?successionis] nem
mindenkor [?…] ellene. [?Örös] törvényünk, hogy örökösnek ne agnoscállyuk; s ha egyszer magunk infringállyuk azon
törvényünket, az után rést hagyván rajta, azon résen [?involálván] minden szabadságinkban, könnyebben eltapodhattya.
Más királyök e consilio törvényessen azon hazának feje által igazgatnak, minket penig törvénytelenül pro arbitrio
németekkel nyomorgatnak. Azért most levén ideje, más confederatusok jó akarattya által a Magyar nemzetnek, hogy régi
szabadságát restabiállya, [?…] [?elveszdítsö] [?segéllyen] a vagy [?nyer]. Mondotta osztán: ezt én tsak pro discursu
hozom elö, [?…] prudentioribus committo; tudom, hogy Magyar Országban is vadnak elmék. [?…] ministe[?…]tiumban
mindenkor így resolválván, a Fényes Porta észrevette a dolgokat, mindazt, mire való a mi itt létünk. Továb mit
[?tevök] legyünk, alázatoson elvárjuk Nagod parancsolattyát, mert ennyire [?…] a Fényes Porta Nagod dolgait, itt
egyebet a heverésnél s költésnél nem [?csekszünk]. Megelégelvén itt való szolgálotunkat, méltóztassék kimenetelünkröl
olly dispositiót tenni s provideálni, had válhassunk tisztességessen a Fényes Portátul. [?Iteratoris] instálunk
Nagodnak, mert kegyelmes urunk, az eddig lett processussal soha Nagodot ez a Fényes Porta valamire nem veszi;
[?…] Nagod magával [?…] [?levelig] [?…] penetrállyák a dolgot, s az emberek szívét is vetik is szemünkre. Ha úgyan Nagod
tractálni akar a Fényes Portával, [?Temesvár] [2] miért az elsö Fényes Portán a végekben nem volt s nincz is Nagod
embere Nándorfejérvári, Temesvári Basáknál? Harminczegyedik Augustus írt Nagod levelén el mindeddig is egyebet nem
vehettünk. A mely levelek [?…]nodiumok itt vannak, a melyek is mind consideratiókra valók, [?tehozzuk] emlékezetiben
Nagodnak kimenetelünk alkalmatosságával. Ebben mihez alkalmaztassuk magunkat? [?…] [?…] kérlet[…] is meg[?…]
[?…] hogy csak egy [?fungus] […]

R823_en.md

# R823, Pápai and Horváth to Rákóczi, 19 December 1706: English

Translation of `R823_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes.

## Translation

[…] the King having gone, we at once made ourselves known, and being admitted here without asking on the twelfth of
this month, we reported our long waiting and asked for the promotion of our affairs. He answered: remember my words
which I said to you before, namely that the Sublime Porte does not willingly converse with you in the presence of the
internuncio; your presence, however, is not unwelcome to the Porte. But it is certain that the Prince is treating anew
with the German: for they have come from Vienna [?…] to confer anew on those propositions which the Archbishop of
Kalocsa presented anew; so if you think it is in the interest of your affairs to stay here longer, no one hinders you.
We said: we have no news of this. He asked whether we had received Your Highness's letter. We said: no, since only the
other day the Pasha of Belgrade's courier [?…], bringing Your Highness's letter as far as Adrianople, 465 [?…] his
master's [?…] returned, and that too [?…] Belgrade they hinder. As we [?…] in discourse, he brought up: see, the
Hungarians ought not to shrink so much from hereditary rule, provided it were under laws; the hereditary kings of
England, Denmark and Sweden are nevertheless subject to laws; Hungary too would bind him by law if he became hereditary
King, and so you would keep the fruit [?…]. We answered: lord, the condition of those countries and kings is
different: each of them is a King from their own nation, rules one coalesced nation, governs lawfully, not lawlessly;
but [?Joseph] is German, and we are Hungarians; he has a great empire besides Hungary; in case of [?succession] we
cannot always [?…] against him. It is our perpetual law that we do not acknowledge a hereditary king; and if once we
ourselves infringe that law, then, a breach being left in it, [?invading] through that breach into all our liberties,
he can more easily trample on them. Other kings govern lawfully by the counsel of the head of that country, but us they
oppress lawlessly at their pleasure with Germans. Therefore, the time being now come, through the goodwill of other
confederates, for the Hungarian nation to restore its old liberty, [?…] lose [?…] help or [?win]. He then said: I bring
this up only for discourse; I leave it to the more prudent; I know there are minds in Hungary too. [?…] in the
ministry, always resolving so, the Sublime Porte has noticed the affairs, all that our being here is for. What we are
to do further we humbly await Your Highness's command, for the Porte [?…] Your Highness's affairs so far that here we
[?do] nothing but idle and spend. Having had enough of our service here, may it please you to make such disposition
and provision for our departure that we may part honourably from the Sublime Porte. We [?again] beg Your Highness, for,
gracious lord, with the procedure so far the Sublime Porte will never take Your Highness for anything; [?…] Your
Highness [?…] they penetrate the matter, and they throw people's hearts in our face. If Your Highness indeed wants to
treat with the Sublime Porte, [?Temesvár] [2] why has Your Highness had no man, first at the Porte, on the frontiers,
nor has one now with the Pashas of Belgrade and Temesvár? Of Your Highness's letter written on 31 August we have
received nothing else so far. The letters [?…] here are all worth considering; we [?shall bring] them to Your Highness's
remembrance on the occasion of our departure. To what should we accommodate ourselves in this? [?…] […]

# R765 — [János Pápai] to Ferenc Rákóczi II, 5 January 1707: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R765_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription, groups above 322 as nulls, 220 = a), word-divided. No interlinear decipherment (the record's clear
passages are not transcribed here). Hungarian and Latin as the figures give them; nothing modernised; codes written as
names. Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[200]` an unread group, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

Azért akar ki legyen az [?…] ezen [?végnek], Nagod méltóságos akarattya [?…] lesz közül rendeltetik, a mint a tö[…]
[200] [?…] ez Lengyel Országi állapotban is nekünk a tiszta igaz hüséggel való engedelmesség levén kötelességünk,
[?…] nem disceptálódunk, mint kellyen ez illyen dolgoknak folyni, hanem magunkat továbbra is Nagod méltóságos
dispositiójára ajánlyuk. A Francia Ország oratorhoz elmentünk Nagod [?méltóságos] parancsolattya szerént, az
hadfogadás iránt insinuatiót tettünk, [?meglendvén] [?…], hogy már nincsen hátra egyéb semmi is, hanem hogy
[?meginlálja] a Királynak, a Fényes Porta megengedi a hadfogadást, impetrállyon pénzt azon szükségre. Felelt [110]:
[?szintén] most írok a Királynak, a mint úgy is [?volt] már az elött is megírtam, hogy azt a mostani Vezér meg[?…]
Lengyel [?…] gedi, csak küldgyön pénzt öfelsége; s [?ötha] változván a Vezér, successora ebben difficilis lenne, is a
pénz megállana itt, avagy könnyebben disponálhatna [120] az embereket pénzel; ki [?rül] hogy a Fejedelem is ír[?jon]
a Királynak, szüks[ég] ez s mive[l] [110] az közönséges hír itt a Török Országöknél is, hogy a Francia Király
békességet kíván. Söt azt mondgyák, az anglusok csak in praeliminaribus is annyit [?…], melyet ingyen [?…] gondoltak
volna; mely végre már egy Francia Ország követ fél esztendötül fogva mulatoz secrete a hollandusoknál, a mi közelebb
a velenczések s [?…] [?…] magok [120] rá vállolván a mediatiót acceptá[110]ttatnak is; a [?…] végre követtyek is
vagyon Angliában, Hollandiában. Mondottuk: uram, a méltóságos Fejedelem eddig alkalmatosság levén a [?…] mindazonáltal
[110] akarván megmutatni sincera addictióját a Királyhoz, folytattya megszünés nélkül [?hadakozását]; félünk,
idvezült Fejedelem Tököli Imre casusátul, a midön [?excludáltatott] [?…] békességböl, holott akkor a Király volt
triu[190]fans [?fel]. Felelt: meus rex est generosissimus, neutiquam faciet ut excludat principem, etiamsi fieret;
[?…] [180] non credo. Abban nincz én kétsége a [?méltóságos] Fejedelem[?…], de néha a szükség s a ministerek
suggestiója a királyök elméjét változtatni szöktak. Felelt: meus rex amat et aestimat principem plurimum; [?…]
[?…] [?…] defunctum principem Tekelium [?…]; etiam si tractabit de pace, aut includet principem, aut dabit
[?…]; [?…] eo tempore plus poterit, a [?…] habebit pacem. Emlékeztünk a portrul is, igérte, impetrál egy passust olly
formán, hogy hatva[n] vagy hetven [?tevét] [?…] Francia Országból, hogy szállíthasson Magyar Országra; annak
praetextusával azon passussal a Basák jó akarattyök által többet is szállíthatna. Ezen dolgot [?Golopali]
communicálván, [?mondotta]: ha a Fényes Portának az az elméje, hogy tractállyon velünk, [?ad]de azonkívül nem reménl.
A Vezér kívánván opinióját [?Golo][00]nak a mostani európai s Moskva Országi állapotokban, már beadta úgy, hogy
többet nem kíván Fényes Portá[?…]ttunk volna, a mint inducálta a Moskva [Ország] ellen való hadakozásra, melyet
elöttünk is megolvasot; is öt Olasz Országul leírván nálunk vagyon, de czifra nélkül el nem küldhettük. De mivel
hirdetik, hogy a svécus Augustussal megbékéllett, úgy a Moskva Országgal nagy confusiót szerez ez is itt az emberek
elméjében, [?ek] mi inclinatiójök volna is; mindazonáltal mi úgy hallyuk, a Moskva Országgal kíván továbbra is
hadakozni a svécus; mi is úgy hirdettyük, de a valóság rövid idön ezt is jobban kinyilatkoztattya. Az eddig Nagodnak
lett administratiónak specificatióját az orator velünk communicálta; igérte, hogy továbbra is communicállya. A pénznek
[?…]ban való cursusát az itt folyó tallérral öszvetenni s arrul ítéletet tenni, avagy az oratortul megérteni szökott
[?ekonomiájök]; nehezen engedi. Követek felöl leendö resolutiójáig a Fényes Portá[?…] kerestünk tolmáczot, meg eddig
nem kaphattunk; mondgya is [?…] uram, hogy nem is kaphatni, hanem az orator igérte, egy Francia Országit, ki egyszersmind
Francia Ország secretáriussa is lehet Nagodnak. Voltunk azon, hogy Brü uramat küldgye ki az orator, de abban nem
succedált intentiónk, holott Brü uram maga offerálta magát. Jelentettük a Havasalföld vaida iránt Nagod intentióját.
Felelt: tudgyátok, ma[?gam] [?…] esztendeje, miulta [?…] vaidát akarom promoveálni azon vaidaságra, a ki most is
itt a palatiumban vagyon, meg is semmire nem mehetek; most is három száz erszény pénzt küldött ide a[?…] [210] vaida,
hogy ezt megfogassa, mely miatt ki sem mer innét menni. Írtam a Királynak, várom öfelsége levelét a Török
Császárnak szóllót azon vaidának tisztiböl való amotiójárul, de nehéz valamit véghez vinni ellene, mivel sok pénzi
levén, azzal megelözi a mi csekély [?opiniónkis]. Az mint hogy meg nincsenek itt a Fényes Portán olly állapotban
Nagod dolgai, Nagod halaszsza továbbra ezen intentióját. Idvezült Fejedelem Tököli [200] [?…] terhes állapottyát
nem kevéssé nehezítette vala a vele elött veszekedés, a mint hogy [?…] egyszer az [200] erdélyi Gyulai Ferenczet
Fejedelemségre hely[02]te nem promoveálta. Itt, kegyelmes urunk, azon vaidaság plus offerenti dabitur; jóllehet már
sokszor tentáltak mások is ezen tisztböl evertálni, de a sok pénzel mindenkor praeveálált, úgy hogy egyszer illy
állapottyában ezer erszény pénzt is fizet. Jobb Nagodnak, ha mi iniuriái vadnak is [?tölem], más alkalmatosb idöre
halasztani s addig dissimulálni, mert a ki útban vagyon, czéllyára siet; vigyázni kel úttyában a korára is, mert abban
is megbotránkozván, néha nagy akadályt szerez itt; mi hozzánk jól nem alkalmaztattya magát; a hopmestere s
kapitihaja is egyszersmind Nagodnak sem lészen kárára, ha inkáb továbbra is az igaz szomszédságot [?…] vele, valamint
azon oláh boerök dolga [240] valamely mélyen gyökerezett a szomszédökkal öszvetütött factio, legyen felö, mely hogy
azon veszekedés el jobban [?…] [?…]. S kérlethez menvén, a híreket vele communicáltuk; [?…] Lengyel Ország[?…], azt is
nem igaz, hogy tractálna Nagod ök[?…] is megmondván; jóllehet emlékeztettük szavaira, a midön mon[dot]ta: non est
ingrata vestra praesentia Portae, [?…] vestra [?…] non libenter conversatur vobiscum in praesentia internuncii; de
csak azt felelte: domini, [?…] resolutionem Portae; [?…] nuper [?…] Fényes Porta vult observare sanctam pac[em]
religiose, a qua nec recedet, nisi adversetur ab altera parte. [?…] iam nunc dico: non estis ingrati, nemo vos
impedit, securi hic [?…] [?…]. Azonnal említé a confoederatusoknak mindenütt szer[?…] Lengyel [?…] progressusök volta
Francia Ország elle[n], Moskva Ország; ha a Francia Ország Turint, Barcellonát majd mind egyszersmind meg nem szállott
volna, [?…] victoriam; javallotta továbbra is a Római Császárral való békességet. Kérdettem, [?…] ember az [?Vainovics]
Nagodé, kérve vagyon had vele? Mint hogy Pápai Gáspár uram szerencsénkre megírta, hogy Vainovics ráczhoz megyen
Banyalukára, Lengyel Ország[?…] levén informatusok, mondottuk: [?…] Nagod híve, de hogy had legyen vele, nem tudgyuk;
azért recognoscáltuk Nagod emberének lenni, valamint kezéhez ne adgya. [?…] Mondotta: domini, veniunt infinite
[?…] Germanis contra Hungaros, et credibile [?…] sunt inimici nostri; tamen Porta faciat [?…] [?…] [?…] propensionis
congrua. [?…] [?elölapafi] Mihály fejedelmet [?…] adhuc Viennae habet [?…] coniugem et cuius filiam [?…] vivit, non
mutavit religionem. Minö, hogy nálunk nélkül is jól tud mindeneket. [?…] Bécsben vagyon erdélyi Bethlen Gergely
leánya, a felesége; a Római Császár tett provisiót hihetö tartása felöl; bizonyossan nem tudgyuk, változtatta-e
vallását vagy sem. Mindazonáltal hallottuk, hogy nem erre, kegyelmes urunk, semmi alkalmatosságot nem adván, hogy
elöhozza; a mint hogy meg eddig az erdélyi követekrül nem is emlékeztünk. De a mi[110] [?…] tapasztaltuk eddig lött
discursusiból, igen estimálván Nagodat, azért hozott illy consideratióra való kérdést elö, hogy továbbra is
terjedne elménk, mint hogy illy állapottyában Nagodnak szüks[ég] minden ellenkezhetö dolgokat consideratióra venni,
ezen állapotban olly élesen kiterjeszteni gondolatit, tanáczkozásit, a mely [?…] lehet, tudván azt is, Nagod a bécsi
udvar idvezült Fejedelem Tököli Imre urunk által akarván dissensiót szerezni a hazában, felö azon factiót továbbra is
ne kívánnya folytatni illy úton. Ezen dolognak circumstantiáiról s consequentiáirul gondolkodván, megjelentettük az
oratornak is: vigyázzon igen titkoson s alattomban, valamint a német udvar ne colludállyon a [?…] ne occasio az ne
törtennyék köztök az erdélyi adózás iránt, a mit proiectált Gróf Bethlen Miklós. Mi is a jó módgyát láttyuk,
czirkálódunk ezen dologban igen caute, tudván azt, salus populi veranda est in primis, et in ea florebit religio, melyet
Nagod bölcs ittenes igazgatásábul s hadakozásábul álla[110]dóbban s jobban reménlhetö, mint másunnan. Jelentettük
az után: Nagod kívánnya az erdélyi követeket beküldeni, azért impetrállyon szabados [?accessust] s passust is nekiek,
mint publicus követeknek. Felelt: [?…] audientia a Vezérnek octava praesentis, választ ad. Kérdezé a [?…] Basa,
mint alkalmaztattya Nagodhoz magát; mondottuk: jól; a mint értettük, commendálta jó emberségérül. Onnan [?…] [65045]
[?…] hoz menvén s kérlet feleletihez képest [?…] sinceritate, kérdeztük töle: várakozzunk-e [?…] [?…]? Mondotta:
ha úgyan tölem tanáczot kértek, igaz sinceritással [2] [?…] Török Császár sokat vártatok, keveset vagy jót; nehéz
úgyan, tudom, a várakozás, de rövid nap a Fényes Portán más emberek s más gubernium levén, a ki a [?dolgotokbüz] is
hozzá szóllyon, lészen olly ember. [?Vainovicz] dolgárul [?…] emlékeztünk neki [270], ha [?…] minket spatiáltok itt,
azt is spatiálhattyátok ott. [?…] a midö[n] [160] elö fordul [?…] mutasd ebben jó akaratodat. Felelt: ne félyen
se[?…] [5] [?…] talám ez akkor a [?…] volna, a midön itt jobban meggyökereznek a dolgok; [?…] [?fievenkenesövel]
rézzel kereskedni jó [?…] is levén itt a réznek [?…], de ha más itt való lészen, találjon Nagod iurisditiójában,
nem levén, [?…] Nagod kártul, s úgy mi is az abbul következendö confusiónak [?…] csekély elménk az a mint itt a
Francia Ország[?…] Spanyol Ország[?…] magok alkalmaztatásokat észreveszszük. [?…] a Király megvallott: [?…] super
Gallinis Gallus [850669] [?…] iuvenilibus [?…] sufficit, verum nunc superatur [?…]. Inkáb a basás ellen nem
oltalmazhat Nagod, míg ideje vagyon; való [?szsza] jobb észt nemzetünk javára jobb Nagod annak alkalmatosságával,
mint sem az Nagod alkalmatosságával be [?kellyék], mivel úgy láttyuk, a Török Országnak igen megvetette a német s ez
a mostani confederatusok progressusai [?…].

R765_en.md

# R765, [Pápai] to Rákóczi, 5 January 1707: English

Translation of `R765_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes. The Latin passages are translated with the rest.

## Translation

Therefore, whoever [?…] for this [?end], Your Highness's gracious will [?…] is ordained, as [?…] [200] [?…] in this Polish
situation too, our duty being obedience with pure and true loyalty, [?…] we do not dispute how such affairs should go,
but further commend ourselves to Your Highness's gracious disposition. We went to the ambassador of France according to
Your Highness's command and made an approach about the recruiting, [?…] that nothing else is left but that he
[?report] to the King: the Sublime Porte permits the recruiting, let him obtain money for that need. He answered [110]:
I am writing to the King just now, as I had already written before, that the present Vizier [?…] Poland [?…]; let His
Majesty only send money; and if the Vizier changed, his successor might be difficult in this, and the money would stop
here, or he could more easily dispose [120] the men with money; [?…] that the Prince too should write to the King; it
is needed, since [110] the common rumour here among the Turks too is that the French King wants peace. Indeed they say
the English, even in the preliminaries, [?…] so much that they would not have thought of it for nothing; to which end
a French envoy has been staying secretly with the Dutch for half a year, which the Venetians [?…] taking the mediation
upon themselves [120] are accepted [110]; to that end they also have envoys in England and Holland. We said: lord, the
gracious Prince, [?…] nevertheless [110] wishing to show his sincere attachment to the King, continues [?the war]
without ceasing; we fear the case of the late Prince Imre Thököly, when he was [?excluded] from the peace, though the
King was then triumphant. He answered: my king is most generous, he will by no means let it happen that he excludes the
prince, even if it were to happen [?…] [180] I do not believe it. We have no doubt of this about the gracious Prince,
but sometimes necessity and the ministers' suggestion are wont to change kings' minds. He answered: my king loves and
esteems the prince greatly; [?…] the late prince Thököly [?…]; even if he treats of peace, he will either include the
prince or give [?…]; at that time he will be able to do more [?…] will have peace. We reminded him of the gunpowder
too; he promised to obtain a passport in such form that sixty or seventy [?camels] [?…] from France could be carried to
Hungary; under that pretext, with that passport, through the Pashas' goodwill, more could be carried. Communicating
this matter to [?Golopali], he [?said]: if the Sublime Porte has a mind to treat with us, [?…] otherwise he does not
hope. The Vizier asking [?…]'s opinion on the present European and Muscovite affairs, he has already handed it in, so
that he wants nothing more from the Sublime Porte [?…], as he urged war against Muscovy, which he also read before us;
we have it written in Italian, but could not send it without cipher. But since they announce that the Swede has made
peace with Augustus, this too causes great confusion in men's minds here [?…]; nevertheless we hear that the Swede
wants to go on making war with Muscovy; we too announce it so, but the truth will soon reveal this better. The
ambassador communicated to us the specification of the administration made so far to Your Highness; he promised to
communicate it further. To compare the course of money [?…] with the thaler current here and judge of it, or to learn
[?their economy] from the ambassador: he hardly allows it. Until the Sublime Porte's resolution about the envoys we
looked for an interpreter, but have not been able to get one so far; [?…] says one cannot get one, but the ambassador
promised a Frenchman, who could at the same time be Your Highness's French secretary. We tried to have the ambassador
send out Mr Brü, but our intention did not succeed, though Mr Brü offered himself. We reported Your Highness's
intention about the voivode of Wallachia. He answered: you know, it is [?…] years since I have wanted to promote [?…]
voivode to that voivodeship, who is now here in the palace, yet I can get nowhere; even now the [?…] [210] voivode has
sent three hundred purses of money here to have him seized, because of which he dares not leave here. I wrote to the
King, I await His Majesty's letter to the Turkish Emperor about the removal of that voivode from office, but it is hard
to accomplish anything against him, since having much money he forestalls our small opinion with it. As Your
Highness's affairs are not yet in such a state here at the Sublime Porte, let Your Highness put off this intention.
The burdensome state of the late Prince Thököly [200] was not a little aggravated by the earlier quarrel with him, as
once [?…] he did not promote [200] the Transylvanian Ferenc Gyulai to the Principality. Here, gracious lord, that
voivodeship goes to the highest bidder; though others have often tried to oust him from that office, he always
prevailed with much money, so that once in such a state he paid even a thousand purses. It is better for Your Highness,
even if he has injuries [?…], to put it off to a more fitting time and meanwhile dissemble, for whoever is in the way
hurries to his goal; one must watch the time on his way too, for being offended he sometimes causes great obstacles
here; he does not accommodate himself well to us; his steward and kapi-kiaya too will not be to Your Highness's harm if
[you keep] true neighbourliness with him rather, as the affair of those Wallachian boyars [240] is some deep-rooted
faction stirred up with the neighbours, [?…] so that that quarrel [?…]. Going to the kiaya, we communicated the news to
him; [?…] Poland [?…], that it is not true either that Your Highness is treating [?…]; though we reminded him of his words
when he said: your presence is not unwelcome to the Porte, [?…] he does not willingly converse with you in the presence
of the internuncio, he only answered: gentlemen, [?…] the resolution of the Porte; [?…] the Sublime Porte wishes
religiously to observe the holy peace, from which it will not withdraw unless it is opposed from the other side. [?…] I
say now: you are not unwelcome, no one hinders you, [you are] safe here [?…]. At once he mentioned the confederates'
progress everywhere [?…] Poland [?…] France against Muscovy; if France had not besieged Turin and Barcelona almost at
the same time [?…] victory; he advised continuing the peace with the Roman Emperor. I asked: is [?…] the man
[?Vainovics] Your Highness's, are there troops with him? As Mr Gáspár Pápai fortunately wrote that Vainovics is going to
the Serbian to Banja Luka, being informed [?…] Poland, we said: [?…] Your Highness's faithful man, but whether he has
troops with him we do not know; therefore we acknowledged him to be Your Highness's man, so that he should not hand him
over. [?…] He said: gentlemen, [?…] come endlessly [?…] from the Germans against the Hungarians, and it is credible
[?…] are our enemies; yet let the Porte do [?…] fitting to [?its] inclination. [?…] Prince Mihály [?…] still has at
Vienna [?…] his wife, and whose daughter [?…] lives, has not changed religion. [?…] that he knows everything well even
without us. [?…] In Vienna there is the daughter of the Transylvanian Gergely Bethlen, his wife; the Roman Emperor has
probably made provision for her keep; we do not know for certain whether she changed her religion or not.
Nevertheless we heard that, gracious lord, giving no occasion for it [?…] to bring it up; as so far we have not even
mentioned the Transylvanian envoys. But what [110] [?…] we have found from his discourses so far, esteeming Your Highness
greatly, he raised such a question for consideration so that our minds might extend further, as in such a state Your
Highness needs to take into consideration all things that could be contrary, and in this state to extend his thoughts
and counsels as sharply as [?…] possible, knowing too that the Viennese court, wishing to create dissension in the
country through the late Prince Imre Thököly, our lord, may not wish to continue that faction in such a way. Thinking
about the circumstances and consequences of this matter, we also reported to the ambassador: let him watch very
secretly and covertly that the German court does not collude with [?…], lest an occasion arise between them over the
Transylvanian tribute, which Count Miklós Bethlen projected. We too see the good way; we go about this matter very
cautiously, knowing that the safety of the people is to be revered first, and in it religion will flourish, which is
to be hoped more lastingly [110] and better from Your Highness's wise government and warfare here than from elsewhere.
Then we reported: Your Highness wishes to send in the Transylvanian envoys; therefore let him obtain free [?access] and
passport for them as public envoys. He answered: [?…] audience with the Vizier on the eighth of this month; he will give
an answer. The [?…] Pasha asked how he behaves towards Your Highness; we said: well, as we understood, he commended his
good humanity. From there [?…] [65045] [?…] going, and according to the answer to our request [?…] sincerely, we asked
him: should we wait [?…]? He said: if indeed you ask my advice, with true sincerity [2] [?…] Turkish Emperor, you have
waited long for little or good; waiting is indeed hard, I know, but in a short time, there being other men and another
government at the Sublime Porte, there will be such a man as will speak to [?your affair] too. About [?Vainovics] [?…] we
reminded him [270]: if [?…] you keep us waiting here, you can keep that waiting there too. [?…] when [160] it comes up
[?…] show your goodwill in this. He answered: do not fear [?…] [5] [?…] perhaps this would be then [?…] when affairs take
deeper root here; [?…] to trade in copper [?…] good [?…] the copper here [?…], but if another here will be [?…] to be
found in Your Highness's jurisdiction, not being [?…] from Your Highness's harm, and so we too [?…] of the confusion
following from it; our small understanding [?…] as we notice here how France [?…] Spain [?…] accommodate themselves.
[?…] the King confessed: [?…] the cock over the hens [850669] [?…] sufficed in youthful [?…], but now he is overcome
[?…]. Rather, Your Highness cannot defend against the pasha while there is time; [?…] better sense for our nation's
good, better for Your Highness on that occasion than to have to [?…] on Your Highness's occasion, since we see that the
German has greatly despised Turkey, and these present confederates' progress [?…].

R784, undated [1707], unsigned

The French ambassador reported that no arguments could bring the Porte to war, though the grandees and the whole Turkish nation incline to it; France means to turn Turkey wholly against the Germans, which would harm Hungary; if Muscovy and Poland do not second it, the Germans will overrun Hungary. A note in another hand asks the Prince to keep it secret and decipher it himself.

R784_reading.md

# R784 — unsigned report to Ferenc Rákóczi II, [Constantinople, 1707]: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R784_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription, with the five split groups read as the image shows them; NOTES), word-divided. No interlinear
decipherment. Spelling as the figures give it; nothing modernised; codes written as names. Marks: `[?word]` partly
carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

A Francia Ország oratori jelentette [?mirtt], hogy semmi ratitiökkal nem inducálhattya Fényes Portát az hadakozásra;
más úton aggrediálta az embereket, a mely az elmúlt hadakozásban is sokat [?használtany], [?…] vadnak már öt száz;
[?anmind] a nagyai az embereknek, a kik öszve esküdtek, hogy egyátallyában effectuállyák, hogy a Török Császárnak
hadakozásra kell resolválni magát, mind hogy az egész Török Ország nemzetnek mind arra vagyon hailandósága. Ha öt
vagy hat ezerre telhetnek, nem sokára ki nyilatkozik ezen dolog. A mint hogy ez sorára nem is hallad, kérdettem az
oratortul: reménlhetni-e ebbü[l] valamit? Mondotta: haszontalan bizony nem költök, mely is sok pénzemben áll. Már ezt
penig Aradon titkoson jelentette: úgy látom, kegyelmes uram, a Francia Országnak továbbra is az elméje, hogy ezt a
Török Országot egészen a német ellen fordítsa, kiböl a Magyar Ország nemzetnek nem haszna, hanem kára következnek. A
Tatár Ország chámnak is megírta s el is küldö [?úte] az orator azon ratiókat, a melyekkel aggrediállya a Fényes
Portát, mivel megírta az orator nak[?…]alságnak: absolute hadakozni kell valamely felé. Mely állapotok consideratiókra
valók levén, akartam Nagodnak sietséggel értésére adni, tudván azt, ha most [?…] [?…] a Moskva és Lengyel Ország nem
secundálván, a német [?…] egészen elboríttya Magyar Országot; akkor talám meg nem is kíván tractálni Nagoddal.
Translation

R784_en.md

# R784, unsigned report to Ferenc Rákóczi II, [1707]: English

Translation of `R784_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes.

## Translation

The ambassador of France reported [?…] that with no arguments can he bring the Sublime Porte to war; he has approached
the men by another way, which was of much use in the last war too, [?…] there are already five hundred; [?all] the
grandees, who have sworn together that they will by all means bring it about that the Turkish Emperor must resolve on
war, since the whole Turkish nation is inclined to it. If they can reach five or six thousand, the matter will soon
come out. As it has not yet come to its turn, I asked the ambassador whether anything could be hoped from it. He said:
I certainly do not spend in vain, and it costs me much money. This, however, he reported secretly at Arad: as I see,
gracious lord, France still has in mind to turn this Turkey wholly against the German, from which not profit but harm
would follow for the Hungarian nation. The ambassador has also written to the Tatar Khan and sent him the arguments
with which he approaches the Sublime Porte, since he wrote to [?…]: one must absolutely make war in some direction.
These matters being worth considering, I wished to let Your Highness know in haste, knowing that if now [?…] Muscovy
and Poland do not second it, the German [?…] will overrun Hungary entirely; then perhaps he will not even wish to treat
with Your Highness.

R757, 16 May 1708 read in part

The Muscovite ambassador's message; the envoy cannot send money from Constantinople; Hentér.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

# R757 — [János Pápai] to Ferenc Rákóczi II, [Constantinople], 16 May 1708: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R757_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription, groups above 322 as nulls), word-divided. No interlinear decipherment (the record's clear passages are
not transcribed here). Hungarian and Latin as the figures give them; nothing modernised; codes written as names.
Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[5]` an unread group, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

A Moskva Ország orator a szerént izente nálam meg: azon magyarök nem voltak, nem is láttam, de ha magokat
insinuállyák, meghalgatom s adök választ nekik, va[?gy] nem; az én hatalmamban vagyon. Innen pénzt el nem küldhetem,
mivel ennek a Fényes Portának religio szerént való canona az, hogy valaki ide jö, admittál[?…] [5] [?…]. A Moskva
Ország orator elött s kérlet által a Fényes Portán Hentér uramat is hívtuk, de nem jött; mi kötelességünket követvén
elmentünk ü kérletéhez. [?Nani] mondotta: dicam resolutionem Fényes Portae [9] [?via] estis coniuncti cum domino
Hentér: [?…] aulae praefectus resolverat, ut vos tres in sui praesentiam admittet, sed distulit illam suam
intentionem; attamen [?…] ad portam, de minus [?…] [9] vi resolvet tihaia; dabimus ad notitiam. Hic modernus aulae
praefectus est vir prudens tum prior est; tum illas [9] [?…] Hentér attulit simul cum propositionibus vestris, de
capite ad calcem percurrit et investigavit. Abovo kérdeztük: [?…] [?…] conductust? Felelt: igen is, de nem [?…]
[?…]. Feleltünk: ita, sed cum armis. Felelt: ita, et non [?…]. A tihaiához menvén a ho[?…] a cancellárius is jelen
volt, kinek köntösét megtsókolván szerenczéltettük mostani uj tisztiben; becsüllessel [?vetésle] ültetett [3000]. Az
után kérdezte Hentér uramtul: a Felséged s status[?ok] [?…] [4] [?vevel] jöttek? Felelt: igen is, akkor a mikor a
Fejedelem inauguráltatott a Fejedelemségre, ü az ország homagiumát letette öfelségének, s úgy öfelsége is megesküdt
a statusoknak. Kérdé ütet: ha most a Fejedelem nem tenne állya [?…] székes helyet Posonol[?…] [?…] más ok birodalmán
kívül? Mi feleltünk: Szebeni nem székes helye a Fejedelemknek, hanem Fejérvár, ahol öfelségének actu generálissa
vagyon maga; öfelsége hüségek[?…] [2] ben vadnak az ország statusi rabutin bemenetele alkalmatosságával; Szebeni,
brassai [?…] [?…] [?Dániábül] engedelmeskedtek a németnek; eo non obstante az egész ország [?statuni] az öfelsége
hüségében vadnak. Proponálta röviden Hentér uram a [?lennis] követeknek admissiójok iránt a Felséged s statusok
kívánságát. Felelt a tihaia: a mely levelet hozta, a Fejedelemtül s az ország statusitól az ök [?…] reá[?…] relegálnak,
hogy szavaidnak hitelt adgyunk; [?…] [?…] a mint most is megértettük, hogy solennis követek jöhessenek. Azt kérdem
meg: is a Fejedelem bírja-e egészen Erdélyt? Hentér uram: azt nem mondhatom, mert a német bírja Brassót, Szebent,
Fogarast. Mi mondottuk: ha [?szab] [?Imperium] szólnunk bövebben ezen dologban, [?szólhatunk] jól tudgya a Fényes
Porta, midön Boczkai, Betlen Gábor s töb fejedel[mek] [3000] az után is recurráltanak a Fényes Portához, minek elötte
egészszen bírták volna is Erdélyt s azon városokat, a Fényes Porta [?annual] s ö kívánságoknak s protegálta.
Felelt [2] [?…] tihaia: bírta a Fényes Porta Budát, [?Morva] mi hitünk [?…] canona [?az], azt tartyök, [?…]
Fejedelemtül acceptállyunk követeket; Fejedelem [?…] [?regnál] más compossessiója [160] kívül, mert egy fejedelemség
abban [?áll], [?…] jurisditiójában legyen az ország absolutum [?dominium]; a [?…] olly követeknek az ö rendes becsületeket
meg kel adnunk, [?emeikben] kel pompával küldenünk [?…]; állopottyában penig az országnak fejedelemnek követtyknek
a becsületet meg nem adhatnánk. Poson messze vagyon; a Fejedelemtül parancsolattya vagy Nándorfejérvári Basának
a Fejedelem [?…] szóval, akar levél által [?correspondeállyon]; Lengyel Ország közel[?…] vadnak egymáshoz; üntelen
a Fényes Portát tudosíts[?…] [?…] a feje [?…] Kassának a Nándorfejérvár Basával [?correspondeál]; kar [?lenni] míg
Isten a [?dolgokat] is jóbra adgya, és mi[?nt] a nekünk mind a Fejedelemnek jobb módunk lészen a beszélgetésben a
Nándorfejérvári Basával. A Fejedelem az hol is ért[?…] a Fejedelemk meghit embere vagyon, s ott is ha mit
proponáltok, meghalgatunk. Ez illy nagy dologban gradatim kel procedálnunk; a Fejedelemk is jobb lészen. Hentér uram
viszsza bocsáttot az [?írul], adatik fermán, magam viszem a Vezér eleibe, procurálom a választ. Felelt: tudgyuk mi azt,
de nem szóllunk, mivel kettönknek további dispositiójáig a Fejedelemnek itt kel maradnunk; recurrálunk mi azzal,
admittállyon igen is; mondgyátok meg nekem, megjelentem a Vezérnek; egy szer [?…] megadnak levél által választ mind
[?valószát]. [?Imperium] lészen pro salvo conductu fermány, [?…] ista resolutione Portae estis contenti? Dabit Deus brevi
meliora. A titkos tractát, de mivel úgy parancsolta, hogy suspendállyuk Felséged intentióját, halgattunk. Magyarázat:
a tihaia láttatott magához kapcolni, ne változnék ez többet. [?Er] a Vezér [?…] [?ezek] [?Bavarial] megítélheti mi
ökbül difficilinkedik a Fényes Porta; ahoz képest moderállya az erdélyi operatióit, úgy a Nándorfejérvár Basával való
operatióit továbbra is. A kiki a tihaiának kedves emberei által is milly [?invitált] ad colloquium titkon szembe
lenni a tihaiával, hogy jobban is [?…] [?…] a tihaia feleletit: nem absolutus Fejedelem, addig míg egészszen nem bírja,
[?látnám], olly létnem agnoscálnak; most nem tudtak egyebet mondani a követek admissiója iránt a Fényes Porta. [?…]
meg [?legiczti] dolgotok, mert lábra indult; nem hijába [?…] tok eddig. A kecskeméti casus [?temfele] [?…] volna, a
Török Császár bizony kezén tartya; ez e [?…] már nem mi, hanem a Római Császár bontotta fel a békességet; a
Nándorfejérvári Basára bízattattak dolgaitok; onnan hidgyétek a Porta valóságos akarattyát. Annyira is elméjére vette
a Fényes Porta dolgotokat, a Török Császár is minden propositiotok szórul szóra eleiben ment írásba, megolvasta
eddig. [?…] sok fáradságtok nem lészen hijába; is tudom a dolgokat, tudtam azt is, mi valósztok lészen a tihaiának,
a kiknek illet, mondottam: illeti az(t) [?…] [?szükis] a Római Császár iránt a Svécia Király Bohemia szélbe való
menetele, [?vél] általvihetünk véghez mind Fényes Porta, mind a magyarökre [?szerü] ez valóság; errül eleve
gondolkozzék Svécia vagy Lengyel Ország [?…], hogy a Fényes Portára jöhessen, a babadaki szerdárnak a Fényes Portárul.
