R757_en.md

# R757, [Pápai] to Rákóczi, 16 May 1708: English

Translation of `R757_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes. The Latin passages are translated with the rest.

## Translation

The ambassador of Muscovy sent me word thus: those Hungarians have not been [to me], I have not seen them, but if they
make themselves known I shall hear them and give them an answer or not; it is in my power. I cannot send money from
here, since by the religion of this Sublime Porte the canon is that whoever comes here is admitted [?…]. Before the
ambassador of Muscovy and through a request at the Sublime Porte we also called Mr Hentér, but he did not come; doing
our duty, we went to his request. [?…] said: I shall tell you the resolution of the Sublime Porte [?…] you are joined
with Mr Hentér: [?…] the steward of the court had resolved to admit you three to his presence, but he put off that
intention; yet [?…] to the Porte [?…] the kiaya will resolve; we shall let you know. This present steward of the court
is a prudent man, more so than the former; Hentér brought those [?…] together with your propositions; he went through
them from head to foot and examined them. From the start we asked: [?…] safe conduct? He answered: yes, but not
[?…]. We answered: yes, but with arms. He answered: yes, and not [?…]. Going to the kiaya, the chancellor was present
too; kissing his robe, we congratulated him on his new office; he seated us with respect [3000]. Then he asked Mr
Hentér: did [?…] Your Majesty and the Estates [?…] come? He answered: yes; when the Prince was inaugurated to the
Principality, he took the country's homage to His Majesty, and His Majesty too swore to the Estates. He asked him:
whether the Prince now [?…] the seat at Pozsony [?…] outside others' realm? We answered: Sibiu is not the seat of the
Princes but [Gyula]fehérvár, where His Majesty's general actually is; the Estates of the country are in His Majesty's
loyalty [?…] on Rabutin's entry, those of Sibiu and Brașov [?…] obeyed the German; notwithstanding that, the Estates of
the whole country are in His Majesty's loyalty. Mr Hentér briefly proposed the wish of Your Majesty and the Estates
about the admission of the [?…] envoys. The kiaya answered: the letter he brought from the Prince and the Estates of the
country [?…] refer to you, that we should credit your words; [?…] as we now understood, that solemn envoys might come.
I ask this: does the Prince hold all Transylvania? Mr Hentér: I cannot say so, for the German holds Brașov, Sibiu,
Făgăraș. We said: if [?…] we may speak more fully in this matter, the Sublime Porte well knows that when Bocskai,
Gábor Bethlen and other princes [3000] afterwards turned to the Sublime Porte, before they held all Transylvania and
those towns, the Sublime Porte [?…] their wishes and protected them. The [?…] kiaya answered: the Sublime Porte held
Buda, [?…] our faith [?…] the canon; we hold that [?…] we accept envoys from the Prince; a Prince [?…] [160] outside
another's co-possession, for a principality consists in the country being [?…] in his jurisdiction as absolute
[?dominion]; to such envoys we must give their due honour and send [?…] with pomp; but in the [present] state of the
country we could not give the honour to the envoys of the prince. Pozsony is far; [let him correspond] by the Prince's
order, either by word or letter, with the Pasha of Belgrade; Poland [?…] are close to one another; let him keep the
Sublime Porte constantly informed [?…] the head [?…] of Kassa [?correspond] with the Pasha of Belgrade; [?…] until God
turns [?affairs] to the better, and both we and the Prince will have a better way of talking with the Pasha of
Belgrade. Wherever the Prince [?…] the Prince's trusted man is, there too, if you propose anything, we shall listen. In
so great a matter we must proceed by degrees; it will be better for the Prince too. Mr Hentér was sent back [?…] a
firman is given, I take it myself before the Vizier, I procure the answer. He answered: we know that, but we do not
speak, since until further disposition for the two of us the Prince requires us to stay here; we have recourse with
that, let him admit [us]; yes, tell me, I report it to the Vizier; once [?…] they will give an answer by letter [?…].
There will be a firman for safe conduct [?…]; are you content with this resolution of the Porte? God will soon give
better. The secret treaty: but since he ordered us to suspend Your Majesty's intention, we kept silent. Explanation:
the kiaya seemed to attach [us] to himself, so that this should not change any more. [?…] the Vizier [?…] may judge
from which the Sublime Porte finds difficulty; accordingly he moderates the Transylvanian operations, likewise the
operations with the Pasha of Belgrade, further on. Through the kiaya's favourites too he [?invited] us to a secret
colloquy with the kiaya, so that [?…] the kiaya's answer: he is not an absolute Prince as long as he does not hold it
entirely; [?…] they do not acknowledge such; now they could say nothing else about the admission of the envoys to the
Sublime Porte. [?…] your affair, for it has got on its feet; you have not [?…] in vain so far. The Kecskemét case [?…]
the Turkish Emperor surely keeps it in hand; [?…] not we but the Roman Emperor broke the peace; your affairs have been
entrusted to the Pasha of Belgrade; from there believe the Porte's real will. The Sublime Porte has taken your affairs
so much to heart that the Turkish Emperor too had all your propositions put before him in writing word for word and has
read them. [?…] your many labours will not be in vain; I know the affairs, I knew too what your [?…] will be to the
kiaya, to whom it pertains, I said: it concerns [?…] the King of Sweden's march to the Bohemian border against the
Roman Emperor, [?…] we can carry it through, both the Sublime Porte and the Hungarians [?…] this is the truth; let
Sweden or Poland think of it beforehand [?…], so that he can come to the Sublime Porte, to the serdar of Babadag, about
the Sublime Porte.

R740, 2 Jan 1710, Constantinople decoded, not read

The Kiaya's answer, in cipher: be patient, do not hurry, wait; if the Porte does not take the matter to heart, Rákóczi should not spend money and effort at the Porte in vain.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

# R740 — János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 2 January 1710: reading of the cipher passage

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R740_read.txt` (envoy key R580 on this project's own
transcription `../R740_transcription.txt`, 168 groups, all in the key), word-divided. The cipher is one passage of the
letter (the Kiaya's answer); the clear words written inside it (`<Felelt:>`, `<en nagy>`, `<Memorialet>`, `<qui>`,
`<vagy>`, `<yak>`, `<mondgya ki>` in the transcription) are put back in place, so the reading is that passage as the
letter gives it. Spelling as the figures give it (*Fgd* =
Felséged, abbreviated in the cipher); nothing modernised; codes written as names. Marks: `[?word]` partly carried,
`[…]` letters that give no word, `[x]` a letter supplied.

## Reading

Felelt: legyen patientiával, ne siessen, várakozzék; én nagy [?…] Bécs[?k] meg [?egynehány] [?…]pot. Az után
Memorialet küldök a Vezérnek, megírván, hogy ennyi esztendöktül fogva lett szíves requisitióit F[elsé]g[?nek], ha a
Fényes Porta szívére nem veszi, vagy [?…] a legyen végsö ajándéka [?…] F[elsé]gdhez; mondgya ki világoson a Fényes
Porta akarattyát, ne kellyen illyen haszontalan költeni s alkalmatlankodni F[elsé]gdnek a Fényes Portán.

R741, 8 May 1710, Constantinople read in part

At the Defterdar's; the Pasha of Belgrade's letter concerned only the merchants' affairs.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (89% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

R741_reading.md

# R741 — János Pápai to Ferenc Rákóczi II, Constantinople, 8 May 1710: reading

Written 23 Oct 2026 (Claude Opus 5.5, verification 4) from `../R741_read2.txt` (decode2.py: envoy key R580 on the DECODE
transcription, groups above 322 as nulls), word-divided. No interlinear decipherment (the record's clear passages are
not transcribed here). Spelling as the figures give it (*Fegd* = Felséged, abbreviated in the cipher; *arnótok*);
nothing modernised; codes written as names. Marks: `[?word]` partly carried, `[…]` letters that give no word, `[x]` a
letter supplied.

## Reading

A teftedárnál. De a Nándorfejérvári Basa levelébe semmi egyéb nem volt, hanem hogy [?…] mindeddig is az kereskedöi
[?…] meg [?…] fizettenek; azért [?…] a parancsolatö. Azon leveleibe csak a kereskedök dolgárul írt, de utánnom küldi
postáját, mely meg az útba elér, az által recommendállya Fe[lsé]g[e]d dolgait a fö Vezérnek. De [?meg] itt sem, mert
a teftedár, mikor megolvasta, mondotta: ezen levél a kereskedök dolgába íráttatott úgyan, de a végre Budáról [?…]
[?…] pasa híre [?…] ide a Fejedelemtül, ki is [?…] a Fe[lsé]gd levelét; én most nem vagyök olly állapotba,
tiszt[?em]be, hogy a fejedelemnek szolgálhossak. Ez a Vezér tihaiája tiszti; hogy most a fö Vezér tihaiája nem vagy,
de úgy a[n]csak belsö embere vagy a Vezérnek, a Basa [?írja], hogy általad folytassuk a dolgokat a Vezér elött. A
Vezért a Fejedelem embere ide jöveteli iránt [?requirálta] is, akkor is csak veled communicálta ezen dolgot. A Vezér
maga kezével [?…] a Basának, hogy küldgyön engem [?…]; de mi dolgotok vagyon a Vezérnél? A Fejedelem segítséget
[?…] kíván; [?…] fel e mehet neki a segítség? [?…] nem láttyuk annak titkos [?…] észre[?…]: ha most Bendertül
egynehány ezer arnótokat a Vezér Moldvába, Campilung felé, olly szín alatt, a Lengyel Országi szélekre vigyáznának,
azon vigyázás színe alatt Máramarosba kevesenként beszivároghatnának birodalmába, levén minden strepitus nélkül
meglehet; nem más országon kel[l]ene [?…] azon hadaknak által menni, [?…] Benderbül Moldvára menne, onnan a
Máramarosi szélekon Campilungon túl Máramarosba. Ez nem lehet, nem a békesség sértödése nélkü[l], mert azok szökve
mennének, tumultust csinálván egymás között, valamint eddig is szöktenek arnótök, bosnyákok közinkbe. [?…] [?…]
ütközhatna a békesség felbo[n]tásával. [?…] Nándorfejérvárt [?…] controvertálna is, legalább ezzel [?…] a
Kecskeméten s másutt lett törökök [?…], s az után ily segítségtek által jobban a mivelünk leendö redintegrálódhatnék
a békesség a Római Császárral, mi is megmaradván, Török Ország is a mi szomszédságunkkal erösödnék a Római Császár
ellen. [?…] Fényes Porta [?…], mióta Fényes Porta protectiója által stabiliálta [?…] az ellenség [?…] az országbul,
bátorságossá teszi [?…] Magyar Ország iránt is idövel; olly szomszéd [?…] lészen dolgotok, kivel nem könnyen
bírtok: kerüllyétek az ellenséget, ha csak kerüllyük s nem [?…] ellene; [?…] olly [?circulust] csinál az [?…]kívül
kerüllyük. Mi tanácsot ad: mennyek a fö tihaiához, s az által insinuállyam magamat a Vezérnek. Akkor is bezárotta az
ajtókat, szolgáinak is meghagyta, vigyázzon, a valaki bejöjjön. [?…] magát [?…] egynéhány napokig a Nándorfejérvári
Basa embere után; ha jö, jó, beadom a Fe[lsé]g[e]d levelét a Vezérnek, de ha látom, semmit nem is reménlek, hallgatok
az ujjab propositiók iránt in generalibus. Kérem, eddig lett requisitióit [?…] Felségednek, a használlyon dolgainkhoz.
Az utánna, ha nem lészen, viszsza megyek, és így meg járom az Aprilist; [?…] szokot [?…] szerént a mi dolgainkrul is
olly balul s hidegen beszél, mint ha jóakarója nem volna nemzetünknek. Hihetö.
Translation

R741_en.md

# R741, Pápai to Rákóczi, 8 May 1710: English

Translation of `R741_reading.md` (23 Oct 2026), same holes.

## Translation

At the defterdar's. But in the letter of the Pasha of Belgrade there was nothing else but that [?…] the merchants have
so far paid [?…]; therefore [?…] the order. In his letters he wrote only of the merchants' affairs, but he sends his
post after me, which will catch me up on the way, and through it he recommends Your Majesty's affairs to the Grand
Vizier. But not here either, for the defterdar, when he had read it, said: this letter was indeed written about the
merchants' affairs, but to the end that from Buda [?…] the pasha's report [?…] here from the Prince, who [?…] Your
Majesty's letter; I am not now in such a position, in my office, that I could serve the Prince. That is the office of
the Vizier's kiaya; though you are not now the Grand Vizier's kiaya, you are still an intimate of the Vizier, the Pasha
[?writes], so that we should carry on the affairs before the Vizier through you. The Vizier [?asked] about the coming
of the Prince's man here, and then too he communicated the matter only with you. The Vizier with his own hand [?…] to
the Pasha that he should send me [?…]; but what business have you with the Vizier? The Prince wants help; [?…] can help
go up to him? We do not see [?…] secret [?…]: if now from Bender some thousands of Albanians [were sent by] the Vizier
into Moldavia towards Câmpulung, under colour of watching the Polish borders, under colour of that watch they could
seep little by little into Máramaros, into his realm; it could be done without any noise; those troops would not have
to pass through another country, [?…] from Bender to Moldavia, from there along the Máramaros borders, beyond
Câmpulung into Máramaros. This cannot be without injury to the peace, for they would go as deserters, making tumult
among one another, as Albanians and Bosnians have deserted to us before. [?…] it would collide with breaking the peace.
[?…] Belgrade [?…] would dispute it, at least with this you could [?…] the Turks killed at Kecskemét and elsewhere, and
then with such help the peace with the Roman Emperor could be better restored with us, and we too surviving, Turkey
would be strengthened by our neighbourhood against the Roman Emperor. [?…] the Sublime Porte, since through the Porte's
protection it established [?…] the enemy [?…] from the country, makes it safe [?…] towards Hungary too in time; you will
have such a neighbour as you will not easily manage: avoid the enemy, if only we avoid him and do not [?…] against him;
[?…] he makes such a circle [?…]. What advice he gives: that I should go to the chief kiaya and through him make myself
known to the Vizier. Even then he had shut the doors and told his servants to watch that no one came in. [?…] some days
for the man of the Pasha of Belgrade; if he comes, good, I shall hand Your Majesty's letter to the Vizier; but if I see
I hope for nothing, I shall keep silent about new proposals in general terms. I ask that his requests so far [?…] Your
Majesty, that it may help our affairs. After that, if nothing comes, I shall go back, and so I shall see out April;
[?…] he speaks of our affairs too so badly and coldly, as if he were no well-wisher of our nation. Probably.

Key: DOC_R581_D2715_2715.txt, DOC_R580_D2713_2713.txt
